Successivamente, a margine della cerimonia di premiazione, il vicepresidente ha aggiunto: “La chiave è proseguire giorno dopo giorno con costanza, facendo gruppo. La coesione è fondamentale per raggiungere gli obiettivi prefissati. Diventerà la futura casa del Napoli (riferendosi al nuovo centro sportivo), un luogo in cui far crescere i giovani, costruire il nostro vivaio e scoprire nuovi talenti. Abbiamo vissuto tantissime esperienze entusiasmanti, dall’era Spalletti fino a Conte. La più grande è stata osservare la squadra capace di conquistare costantemente traguardi prestigiosi e di mantenersi sempre ad altissimi livelli”.

#2163. West Ham, Millwall and Norwich City among clubs interested in League Two loan star

Clubs up and down the country will be preparing for the January transfer window, and among those likely to be active are Championship trio West Ham, Millwall and Norwich City. It has been said that WHUFC boss Scott Parker is prioritising a new striker and a left-back, but TEAMtalk states the club have eyes on centre-back Jack Taylor.

It is reported that the Hammers are alongside the Canaries and arch-rivals Millwall in tracking Taylor, who has thrived on loan at Exeter City from Stevenage this season. A host of clubs scouted the 21-year-old in action against Huddersfield Town earlier this month – a game in which he scored.

Taylor has Premier League admirers too though, with Brighton & Hove Albion, Everton, Brentford, Crystal Palace and Fulham taking an interest. Clubs are considering winter moves with the possibility of keeping him on loan at Exeter City for the remainder of the season.

Taylor, born in Bexhill-on-Sea in 2005, has risen through non-league to the EFL. He started out in the Eastbourne Borough setup before moving to Sutton United, and a string of loans in the lower leagues ultimately earned him a first-team spot at Gander Green Lane.

Stevenage came calling in January 2026, but limited opportunities prompted his summer loan to Exeter City. The 21-year-old has had to wait for his chance, but Taylor has now started the last eight League Two games on the trot. The Grecians have won five of those and kept five clean sheets in the process.

Taylor’s per 90 stats show just how effective he has been since breaking into Matt Taylor’s side. And, it is little wonder that sides like West Ham, Millwall, Norwich City and Premier League clubs are taking note.

No League Two centre-back betters Taylor’s 17.27 defensive contributions per 90, and he ranks top for his 79.2% duel win rate as well. He also averages 6.24 aerial duel wins, 11.51 clearances and 2.69 tackles per game (all stats via FotMob).

It isn’t often that young players rise through non-league and reach high levels anymore, such is the level of competition throughout the English pyramid. So, for Taylor to emerge as he has and draw interest from top clubs speaks volumes about his potential.

However, given his steep trajectory and age, admirers like West Ham, Millwall and Norwich City will have to be patient with his development. Taylor could yet benefit from seeing out a full season in League Two or playing some League One football before moving to the Championship – or even the Premier League.

Unfortunately for Exeter City, they are passengers when it comes to a decision over Taylor’s future. The call will be Stevenage’s to make, and they may well be inclined to cash in if the opportunity arises amid interest from WHUFC and more.

#2161. Kean è un caso per il Como, che non vuole l'operazione. La scelta per la lista Champions

Il Como attende novità sulle condizioni di Moise Kean, che è diventato un vero e proprio caso. 35 milioni di euro tra parte fissa e bonus la cifra spesa per acquistarlo, ma il giocatore fino ad ora è stato frenato da diversi fastidi fisici alla tibia. Le indiscrezioni hanno riportato un duro contrasto di gioco riportato in una seduta al centro sportivo di Mozzate. E il club ha scritto: "A seguito di un problema verificatosi durante l’allenamento, il calciatore Moise Kean si sta sottoponendo a ulteriori accertamenti medici. La priorità del Club è sostenere Moise nel suo percorso di recupero".

Rimasto ko per una botta ricevuta nel match contro il Belgio con la Nazionale italiana nella sosta di settembre-ottobre, è rimasto fuori inizialmente una settimana e mezza, per poi vedersi protrarre i tempi di recupero di oltre un mese. Una capatina a Barcellona, all’istituto Cugat, per svolgere un trattamento mirato alla risoluzione del fastidio alla tibia. Ora è comparso un ulteriore intoppo lungo il percorso di recupero, ma secondo le informazioni raccolte da TuttoMercatoWeb la volontà da parte del Como sarebbe di non arrivare a un intervento chirurgico. Preferita una via conservativa, in attesa degli esiti degli esami strumentali dopo quanto accaduto in allenamento.

Per il ko riportato da Jesus Rodriguez (di oltre 60 giorni), il Como ha effettuato un cambio liste Champions per permettere l'inserimento di Maxence Caqueret nell'elenco dei convocati di Fabregas per gli impegni europei - i prossimi contro Betis Siviglia, PSG e Barcellona. Tuttavia, stando a quanto appurato, questo non avverrà per Kean: l'attaccante italiano resta segnato e non verrà sostituito da un altro giocatore. Questo anche perché è possibile sostituirne uno solo di giocatore fino alla sesta giornata di League Phase compresa. #2158. Derby County will hope influential star can build on Bolton Wanderers display and hit form after a tough season: Opinion

Derby County remain rooted in the Championship relegation zone after a tough season to date, but they enjoyed a much-needed win over Bolton Wanderers before the break. Not only did the 3-1 triumph mark the end of a 10-game winless streak for DCFC, but it might just have kept John Eustace in his job.

Ryan Nyambe made headlines for scoring his first career goal in the win, while Jack Robinson and Rhian Brewster also got on the scoresheet. They were among the standout players against the Trotters, while Mo Fuseini and Jacob Widell Zetterstrom also impressed.

Carlton Morris was another man to play a central role in the win. The Rams striker has come under fire amid his poor form at the start of the 2026/27 season, but after a much-improved display against Bolton Wanderers, Morris will be eager to kick on and get back to his dangerous best in front of goal.

Although some have overstepped the mark, criticism of Morris has been justified this term. He hasn’t been helped by a Derby County side desperately lacking in creative spark and quality in possession, but one goal in 12 games across all competitions marks a big drop-off from last season.

His sole goal has come from only 2.38 xG in the Championship too, so not only has he struggled to convert his opportunities, he isn’t getting many high-quality chances either (FotMob).

Morris’ wait for a first Championship goal since August continued against Bolton Wanderers, but the all-round play of the 30-year-old striker was far better. It was his header across the box that Robinson scored the second goal from. And, after he held up the ball well, his cross was flicked onto Nyambe by Fuseini for the equaliser.

The DCFC man created three chances in total and won 11 aerial duels (FotMob). He was tireless in attack for Eustace as they fought to secure a key win, and it showed why the boss has retained faith in Morris.

There have been criticisms about his mobility from supporters. But, in Morris, Eustace has a powerful outlet at the top of the pitch who will run and battle for his team. He leads from the front, and if he can rediscover his goalscoring touch, he can play a key role in turning around the Rams’ fortunes.

Morris’ display against Bolton Wanderers showed how he brings other players into the game and offered a timely reminder of how his aerial ability and physical traits can be used with better service.

But, ultimately, Morris needs to start scoring to really silence doubters and keep his place. Lars-Jorgen Salvesen has found game time hard to come by but his two goals equate to 0.75 goals per 90. It has raised fair arguments that the Norwegian should be given a starting chance over Morris.

Morris scored 12 goals in 31 games last season and he was excellent before injury ended his campaign early. His Bolton Wanderers performance was a much-needed reminder of just how influential he can be, and it can be a catalyst for the striker as he looks to inspire DCFC’s rise away from the Championship relegation zone.

#2157. De Laurentiis: “Film sul Centenario? De Giovanni ha scritto la sceneggiatura, Amazon lo porterà nel mondo

Aurelio De Laurentiis, presidente del Napoli, è stato tra i protagonisti della prima edizione del premio “Eccellenza Calcistica Italiana”, manifestazione dedicata alle personalità che si sono distinte nel panorama calcistico nazionale. L’evento si è svolto a Villa Campolieto, a Ercolano, dove il patron azzurro ha ricevuto un riconoscimento nella categoria Fair Play, che vedeva tra i finalisti anche Andrea Belotti e Luca Percassi. Nel corso del suo intervento, De Laurentiis ha parlato anche del film dedicato al Centenario del Napoli: “La sceneggiatura del film del centenario la scrive il qui presente Maurizio de Giovanni. Il calcio in Inghilterra è nato con gli aristocratici. Non era un fatto popolare. Arrivarono a Napoli e iniziarono a giocare con gli scugnizzi. Il 1922 sembrerebbe la vera data, poi procrastinata al 1926 per la nascita del Napoli. Che non si chiama Società Sportiva, ma Calcio Napoli. Questo film è venuto talmente bene che una piattaforma importante come Amazon lo porterà nel mondo. Quando abbiamo vinto lo Scudetto con Conte, ho fatto un film e sono andato a Los Angeles: presi questo cinema e ho fatto questa anteprima dove sono arrivati questi tifosi dall’America. C’erano donne emozionatissime che dicevano di non capire nulla di calcio ma che si sono comunque fatte trascinare. Ho scoperto che a San Pedro ci sono 30mila tifosi del Napoli legati all’isola d’Ischia”.

#2153. Barnsley vs Sheffield Wednesday predictions lodged as the Tykes ‘saddening’ decline continues

Barnsley come into Friday’s Yorkshire derby against Sheffield Wednesday in dire straits. The Tykes sit 23rd in the League One table with just nine points to their name after seven defeats in their last nine matches in the third tier.

Daniel Stendel‘s side had found some respite in domestic competition with wins over Aldershot Town and Oldham Athletic in the FA Cup and EFL Trophy respectively. However, their league woes continued with a 2-1 defeat away at fellow strugglers Wigan Athletic last Saturday.

As for Sheffield Wednesday, they claimed a valuable three points in Dave Challinor’s first League One game last weekend. Billy Mitchell, Davis Keillor-Dunn and Di’Shon Bernard scored on the way to an important 3-0 win over Plymouth Argyle, marking the Owls’ first league victory in four matches.

SWFC have to get back on track on the road though, having lost back-to-back games against Notts County and Doncaster Rovers by 2-0 scorelines as Henrik Pedersen‘s tenure came to an end. Wednesday now sit 5th, six points away from the automatic promotion spots.

“Barnsley and Sheffield Wednesday are in drastically contrasting positions. The Tykes are lingering in the League One relegation zone with continued discontent towards the ownership, while SWFC are hoping to challenge for promotion as they rebuild under ambitious leadership.

“Daniel Stendel marked an exciting summer appointment at Oakwell but he has been completely hung out to dry. The decline in quality of that squad is just saddening, and with 24 goals conceded this season, I have little faith that their defence can curtail the visitors’ quality this Friday.

“SWFC are finding their feet under Dave Challinor, but the 3-0 win over Plymouth Argyle marked an exciting result in his first league game. Davis Keillor-Dunn is in fine form and Di’Shon Bernard is a true Championship quality centre-back, and a big asset at this level.

“Perhaps the occasion can motivate Stendel’s young side and drive them to a surprise result. However, you can’t help but feel the visitors win in pretty comfortable fashion at Oakwell.”

“Barnsley are in serious danger of being relegated this season. Their form has been abysmal at home and with no clean sheets at Oakwell this term, they will be up against it on Friday.

“It’s a great opportunity for the Tykes to incite revenge on the Owls who beat them in the play-off final three years ago. However, the difference in squad talent is a lot greater this time around.

“Sheffield Wednesday have been terrific on the road and with an average of 1.67 points a game, promotion is a real possibility. However, two losses away from home in a row should give a glimmer of hope to Stendel’s side.

“I can’t see the hosts winning this one, though, and Challinor will be eager to continue his winning start as boss. I think SWFC will be far too strong on the night.”

#2152. Antonio Conte esce allo scoperto: "Tornare alla Juventus? Prima o poi il cerchio si chiuderà"

Antonio Conte esce allo scoperto e parla apertamente di un suo ritorno alla Juventus. In una anticipazione di un'intervista rilasciata a Cronache di Spogliatoio - la versione integrale uscirà venerdì - è tornato a parlare del suo rapporto con i bianconeri e della possibilità di tornare sia nel futuro: "Penso che prima o poi il cerchio si dovrà chiudere. Io so di aver lasciato qualcosa, so che quel cerchio prima o poi lo dovremo chiudere con la Juventus", la candida ammissione di Conte.

Conte ha raccontato in particolare che ancora oggi molti tifosi della Juventus lo fermano per strada chiedendogli di tornare alla guida della loro squadra del cuore: "Tante volte incontro dei tifosi juventini che mi chiedono: «Quando torni?»", ha raccontato l'ex ct della Nazionale. E poi, ha spiegato: "La risposta è che tornerò quando ci sarà l'opportunità, quando si incroceranno alcune situazioni...". Conte ha anche aggiunto: "So che non sarà semplice, anche se penso che prima o poi il cerchio si dovrà chiudere".

"Ci sono stati in passato dei momenti in cui ci saremmo potuti riunire, ed è molto difficile perché devono capitare due situazioni: io devo essere libero e la Juventus deve cercare un tecnico senza panchina. Ci sono state due situazioni: una quando la Juventus decide di prendere Maurizio Sarri, io ero libero e infatti poi vado all'Inter. La seconda volta che ero libero quando arriva Giuntoli, che poi prende Thiago Motta e io vado a Napoli".

"Dopo Bari potevo diventare l'allenatore della Juventus, avevo parlato con Castagnini e Secco più volte. C'era Ranieri sulla panchina della Juventus, poi lo mandano via e prendono Ferrara. Loro sapevano che avrei fatto il 4-2-4 , avendo Amauri, Iaquinta, Trezeguet e Del Piero dissi che eravamo a posto davanti, che dovevamo prendere esterni. Non so perché presero Diego dal Werder Brema. Mi chiamarono, quel giorno c'era la finale di Champions United-Barcellona a Roma, mi dissero che dovevo andare allo stadio per dare delle rassicurazioni. Io vado, entro da allenatore della Juventus, finisce la partita e mi dicono che dobbiamo discutere. Mi chiedono come volevo giocare e io ripeto 4-2-4. Al che mi chiedono dove avrei messo Diego. Dissi attaccante a fianco alla punta, ma loro dissero che lui voleva fare il 4-3-1-2. Pensa la mia follia, che fa capire come io sia una persona dritta che non scende a compromessi. Un altro avrebbe firmato, passando dal Bari alla Juventus... Lui mi dice questo, io forte delle mie convinzioni e idee, del fatto che non voglio promettere qualcosa che non darò mai dissi: "Se il giocatore deve decidere come dobbiamo giocare noi, non sono l'allenatore giusto per voi". Esco di lì e non ero più l'allenatore della Juventus…".

#2151. Watford’s midfield struggles will continue until reinforcements are signed – here’s three January targets they must consider

A major issue for Watford this campaign has been the midfield. The summer departure of Imran Louza was a major blow, with his technical qualities, set-piece delivery and ability to dictate games sorely missed.

Similarly, Giorgi Chakvetadze’s transfer to Udinese has left the Hornets devoid of a creative spark, with his tendency to drift centrally and unlock defences. The club have simply not recruited adequate replacements, and they are suffering for it.

The current midfield options at Vicarage Road consist of Hector Kyprianou, Edo Kayembe, Edoardo Bove, and recent signing Martin Payero. None of these options are particularly offensive or astute going forward, lacking the ball-carrying and forward-thinking impetus of Chakvetadze, and the game-changing potential of Louza.

In order for Watford to change their fortunes this season, the club’s hierarchy need to address the glaring issues in midfield. Here, we put forward three targets the Hornets should consider moving for ahead of the January transfer window.

Patrick Kelly had a breakout season for Barnsley in 2025/26, establishing himself as one of League One’s most exciting prospects. He was touted for a potential step up, but stayed put and has remained a standout at Oakwell.

A busy and energetic player, Kelly could well offer Watford the spark they need in midfield. His excellent dribbling and physical attributes would complement a side in need of inspiration and dynamism. He ranks among the top 10% of players in his position League One for dribbles completed (FotMob).

Barnsley have had a disastrous start to the current campaign, and as such, a cut-price deal for the 22 year old could tempt clubs further up the footballing ladder.

Bate has been a critical part of highflying Stockport County’s success this season. The diminutive playmaker has contributed four assists and started all but one of the Hatters’ league games this campaign. He excels with his technical ability, ball retention and progression, and eye for a pass.

Having put behind him his injury issues from last season, he appears to have recaptured his impressive form from the 2024/45 campaign, cementing himself as a potential coup for alert Championship sides.

At 24 years of age, he represents a good blend of experience and potential to improve further. His position as club captain further demonstrates the leadership qualities necessary to navigate a Championship relegation scrap, which may await at Watford.

Hull City’s promotion and busy summer window has left players such as Kieran Dowell with limited minutes, opening the door to a potential exit. Dowell has been omitted from the Tigers’ Premier League squad, and the prospect of a January exit appears increasingly likely.

Dowell has donned the colours of six different Championship clubs, and has developed a reputation for scoring spectacular goals, as well as being an apt creator and technician. With three promotions at this level, he has the necessary pedigree and experience.

Out of contract in the summer, Dowell would represent a cost-effective option on either a short-term deal or a loan. His lack of playing time in recent seasons is a potential concern, but should he regain match fitness quickly he could be a useful addition to a threadbare Watford midfield.

As Watford seek to steer clear of the Championship’s lower reaches, bolstering the midfield is of high importance. The only summer addition in this area, Martin Payero, has been underwhelming when given the opportunity.

The fitness issues plaguing Edoardo Bove, the most talented player of the current options, has further stunted Watford’s progress.

The January window offers the club a chance to make amends for poor summer transfer business and to provide the squad with the best opportunity to consolidate their Championship status. The three midfield options put forward would help to push the club in the right direction.

#2150. Millwall controversy takes fresh twist - West Ham will be watching on

A fresh twist has emerged concerning the off-pitch drama at Millwall involving once highly-rated winger, Benicio Baker-Boaitey.

The Lions' start to the Championship season has been one of mixed fortunes, with Alex Neil's side sat 16th, having accumulated 18 points 15 matches.

Neil, one of the second tier's most seasoned managers, has been without several key names so far this season due to injury and suspension issues, which hasn't helped his side's wish for consistency.

One of the players missing in action for quite some time now is Baker-Boaitey, with it initially envisaged that the 22-year-old was also on the treatment table for a prolonged period.

However, recent developments have shed new light on the winger's situation, and it is one which could be about to get worse.

Baker-Boaitey arrived at Millwall in January 2025 after a spell with Premier League outfit, Brighton and Hove Albion, where he made six top-flight appearances for the Seagulls under Roberto De Zerbi after debuting against Chelsea in December 2023.

He would then link up with Port Vale for the 2024/25 League Two season, before his gametime in North Staffordshire was cut short due to injury, with Neil admitting after his move to SE16 was completed that Baker-Boaitey was suffering with pubic symphysis.

Almost two years later, though, the winger is yet to make a first-team appearance for the Lions, and was recently seen posting cryptic Instagram posts, one of which was captioned: "On my mumma, can't even train with the 21s."

His account has since been deactivated, and it has since been widely reported that Millwall are now undertaking disciplinary action, which could even result in the termination of his contract, whilst Baker-Boaitey is also said to be weighing up his options.

Given Brighton's recent ability for producing and unearthing several gems, there was an understandable hype when Millwall added Baker-Boaitey to their squad so soon after the club-record sale of Romain Esse - who has since returned to the club on loan - to Crystal Palace.

“Another England youth international, Benicio ticks a number of boxes we’re looking to recruit for at his young age of 21," said Lions director of football, Steve Gallen.

A Home Office study has revealed which fanbases have seen the most arrests in English football, with those of some EFL clubs ranking high

"He is an exciting winger who will add plenty of pace and skill to the squad, which will get fans off their seats."

However, this prediction has failed to come true, with Baker-Boaitey only making one appearance for the club's Under-21s side shortly after moving from the AMEX Stadium.

Millwall and West Ham United fans' disdain towards one another is certainly well-documented, and was displayed at The Den two months ago in a compelling 2-2 draw.

However, those from East London will certainly have one eye on how their rivals' disciplinary investigation into Baker-Boaitey's conduct plays out.

Indeed, the former England youth international came through the famous academy at Rush Green, and by 2020, aged 16, was linked with a move to Bayern Munich after rejecting professional terms at the London Stadium, eventually signing for FC Porto, with West Ham receiving £190,000 in compensation.

Millwall and West Ham fans, what do you make of the saga involving Benicio Baker-Boaitey? Comment below!

Millwall will be hoping that this doesn't cause any form of distraction regardless of Baker-Boaitey's distinct lack of involvement since joining the club, whilst further impressions could be made on West Ham fans, even if returning to form under new head coach, Scott Parker, is their top focus right now.

#2149. Swansea City backed for Roberto Martinez or Pablo Hernandez move

Swansea City have had to take a double-hit in recent weeks, with a dip in form and interest in their manager from elsewhere, but FLW's Swans fan pundit is optimistic over the identity of his replacement.

Swansea City's ascent to the top of the Championship was one of the surprises of the start of the season in the second tier. Seven wins from their first ten games took them to the top of the pile, while other below them struggled to find the consistency required to dislodge them.

That, however, all came to a juddering stop in the month building up to the international break. Four defeats in their last five have dropped them to sixth place in the table, and there was more bad news for them with reports that head coach Vitor Matos is in the sights of Ligue Un club Strasbourg.

With Matos's departure for France imminent, Swansea are in the hunt for a new manager, and with the top half of the Championship table looking extremely tight this season and tricky matches coming up, whoever the new incumbent is will need to get the team back on form to build on their excellent start.

Football League World have been in contact with our Swans fan pundit Will Hughes about the changing of the guard at The Swansea.com Stadium, and in particular about the possibility of the former West Bromwich Albion manager Carlos Corberan coming to his club, and Will believes that his club currently enticing enough to attract that calibre of manager: "I think Corberan would be good. He's certainly a name that should be floating around there."

Will believes that his club is a more attractive option as a result of changes behind the scenes over the last couple of years: "Honestly, I don't know who Matos's replacement is going to be. I'm not sure how big our pull is now, but I'd definitely say that the Swans job is a lot more desirable now managers now than what it was this time last year, for where we are in the league, the outside noise with over goings-on at the club, with the ownership of Modric and Snoop Dogg, and the fact that we've got more finance now. We've got a better squad. It's definitely a more desirable club."

Will is setting his hopes high over the identity of the next Swansea boss, with one former manager being someone he'd love to see back at his club: "This time last year I certainly wouldn't have been saying some of the names that are floating about now. One name that's been floated by Swans fans has been Roberto Martinez. I would love it if he came home, but dare I say maybe he's a bit above us after managing Portugal and Belgium, two very high profile jobs. Would he see this as a step down? Maybe he doesn't want to ruin his legacy here. I can't see it happening, but I'd love it."

And he had another suggestion with a Swansea connection who he feels could be a realistic target for his club: "Pablo Hernandez has been noted. Castellon are in the same bracket as us under Jamestown Analytics, Tony Bloom's company. I don't know whether they have a clause that we can't import another Jamestown club's manager, but going by the outside noise and the statistics that we see from Castellon, he could be a good fit for us."

Will sees the replacement as potentially an opportunity for his club, and is grateful to Matos for his role in improving Swansea's profile: "I don't whether we'd be looking higher. For me, I'd love it if we upgraded on Matos. Matos came in and he did his job. When he came in, Swans were floundering near the bottom. He kept us up and got us to a mid-table finish, which is what we were expecting, and now this year we've had a really good start, he's got us in the play-offs, near the top of the division. Whether he'd have kept us there we'll never know, but he got us to that point so he deserves credit for making the job more desirable."

Who would you like to see as the new Swansea City manager? Let us know in the comments below!

Our Swansea correspondent is also aware of the importance of the new manager stepping straight in and getting the team back on track: "So the question is can we get someone to keep us there or maybe take us a little bit further up. You'd want someone who's going to come in and hit the ground running. I know it's not as easy as that, but you don't want to bring in someone where you're thinking, "Maybe it'll work, maybe it won't." You want to upgrade. I don't know how far our pull is now, with Jamestown on board, whether we can look at the bigger leagues, at clubs and managers we wouldn't have over the last few years. There's a few out of work. You've mentioned Corberan, I've mentioned Roberto Hernandez. Kieran McKenna would be good, but I've heard we're priced out on him."

And he has one particular fixture in mind, which is coming up on the 29th November: "It's typical that the new manager's first game is probably going to be the South Wales derby. Hopefully it'll be another Garry Monk masterclass; 3-0 in his first game. But we'd hope it doesn't go the way it did afterwards towards the end of his tenure, because it turned sour in the end. I keep saying we'll wait and see. I'd an expect an announcement today that Matos has gone, but we've got Leon Brittan and Chris O'Leary, two legends, looking after us for two games and you'd think hopefully we'll get a new manager in next week, and we can go from there."

Will feels that while the Cardiff game is extremely important, Swansea's season won't be defined by his team's matches against these opponents: "All I would say to Swans fans is not to get on the new manager's back if we don't get the result we want against Cardiff. As much as we all want to beat them, it's a marathon not a sprint. We may lose to them but beat them away from home. Let's not jump on the new manager's back, or Leon Brittan or the club if we don't win that game. It's not the end of the world. It may feel like it, believe me. I've got into some stinking moods when we've lost to Cardiff in the past."

And he believes that the best thing that his fellow supporters can do is get fully behind the team, no matter who the opponents: "But whoever's in charge, let's not get on their back. It was different when we had someone like Michael Duff or Luke Williams when they lost their last South Wales derbies because of the situations that were going on, Luke Williams flirting with West Brom and Michael Duff saying what he said about the derby, so that's fair enough. But let's not get on their back, let's get behind them and hopefully start with a win on Saturday. It's a tricky game against West Brom, but forget about the fact that we haven't got a manager or where they are in the league, it's a game we've got to try and win to stop the rot of our poor form."

Whoever it is who ends up filling the shoes of Vitor Matos at The Swansea.com Stadium will have one immediate job; to turn around the form of a team who were flying at the start of the season. Given that Swansea City haven't finished above 10th place in the Championship in the previous five years, few were expecting them to get near the top of the table this time around, but they managed this on the 1st September and stayed there for most of the next six weeks.

The names mentioned in connection with the vacant position all have something to recommend them. Carlos Corberan has Championship experience from spells with Huddersfield Town and West Bromwich Albion. Pablo Hernandez has a previous Swansea connection from having played there from 2012 to 2014. And Kieran McKenna built a strong reputation from taking Ipswich Town from League One to the Premier League.

And whoever the identity of the new Swansea manager might be, he'll have a tough start. West Bromwich Albion are the Swans' next opponents, and they're one place above them in the table. Next after that come Middlesbrough, who are in second place in the table. And then comes the big derby game against Cardiff City.

So the question facing the club is that of which was the blip? Were Swansea City just on an explosively good run of form at the start of the season which is now settling towards where most people would have put them in the summer, or did Vitor Matos build a squad that is capable of going the entire gruelling length of a 46-game season near the top of the table?

Whichever the answer to that question turns out to be, what is undeniable is that Swansea City demonstrated their potential over the opening two months of the season. If the new Swansea manager can capture that form again, the Swans could yet find themselves pushing to end their nine-year exile in the Championship by its end.

#2148. 3 strikers West Ham should target in January as Scott Parker sets new talisman demand

West Ham have the busy December schedule to see to before the January transfer window comes around, but Scott Parker is already intent on improving his squad. And, he’s identified two areas where he wants to sign new players: striker and left-back.

The centre-forward has been a position of contention for WHUFC, despite the fact they are the league’s top scorers with a remarkable 37 goals in 15 games. None of Joel Piroe, Pablo or Taty Castellanos have proven they can find consistent goalscoring form, and the latter reportedly wants to leave the London Stadium.

With that in mind, we put forward three strikers West Ham should target to resolve their dilemma.

Parker is said to prefer Championship experience, so who better than someone who is already starring in the division? Andri Gudjohnsen has been in scintillating form for Blackburn Rovers, with a goals per shot rate of 0.50 ranking him among the most clinical finishers in Europe this season.

The 24-year-old has scored 11 goals in 15 games this term and has starred on the international stage for Iceland too. Gudjohnsen scored four in two games over the November break.

A fast and powerful forward with smart movement and finishing, Gudjohnsen could come in and lead the line for West Ham and he’d be backed to make the step up to the Premier League too. It would not be a cheap signing, but he could be just what Parker needs.

Looking slightly further afield, one man who could catch the Hammers’ eye is Paulos Abraham. The 24-year-old was highly rated in his youth but a spell at Dutch side FC Groningen didn’t go as hoped, resulting in his return to Sweden with Hammarby IF.

Since returning to the Allsvenskan in January 2025, he has been one of the league’s most dangerous forwards. 17 goals in 28 league games this season take Abraham to 34 goals in 74 outings for the club, and he was rewarded with his first Sweden call-up earlier this season.

Abraham would bring electric pace to West Ham’s frontline, and his inventive finishing sees him score from unlikely situations. His ability to run beyond Championship defences could bring a new threat to a highly creative WHUFC outfit.

Last but not least is certainly the most ambitious of the lot. However, Aleksandar Mitrovic has had a slightly disrupted recent spell in the Middle East due to a ligament injury and may be tempted to return to London under the lead of Parker again.

The Serbian powerhouse played and starred under the new West Ham boss at Fulham, and he would be a dream addition for Parker. Mitrovic is one of the most prolific forwards in Championship history with 85 goals in 126 games at the level and he would certainly fit the bill as a prolific scorer who can lead the Hammers to promotion.

The main obstacle is obvious though. Mitrovic is on massive money in Qatar with Al-Rayyan, and even if WHUFC can persuade the club to let him leave, the 32-year-old might not be minded to leave his sizeable contract.

But, if the opportunity was there, Mitrovic would be a perfect solution for Parker’s striker issue.

#2147. The EFL Championship's top 9 wealthiest owners in 2026/27

The 2026-27 Championship is firmly underway and it's as intriguing as ever.

And with two more play-off spots opening up for the 2026-27 season, the chase for the Premier League riches is more hotly-contested than ever in the second tier of English football.

New financial measures are also coming into place, with profit & sustainability rules (PSR) being taken away and incoming are squad cost rules (SCR), where owners can only invest £33 million in equity over a three-year period, whilst player and manager costs can only amount to 85 per cent of a club's yearly turnover.

Owners tend to need deep pockets to fund a promotion-chasing club in the Championship - but who are the nine wealthiest owners in the second tier ahead of next season? FLW takes a look...

For the longest time, Norwich City were ran by celebrity chef Delia Smith and her husband Michael Wynn-Jones, having joined the club's board in 1996 before becoming majority shareholders in 1998.

Seeking outside investment though for someone with far deeper pockets than themselves, enter Mark Attanasio, who after initially being appointed as a director at Carrow Road in 2022, became the majority shareholder in 2024 - much like how Smith and Wynn-Jones came to being in control 26 years prior.

With prior experience and ownership at Baseball side the Milwaukee Brewers, Attanasio was seen as an ideal fit to take Norwich to the next level, and he now owns 85 per cent of the Norfolk outfit - with a net worth of $1.9 billion (£1.4 billion), according to Forbes, the 68-year-old certainly has the cash to try and take them back to the Premier League.

Blackburn Rovers are into their 16th year of the Venky's ownership, and to say it's been turbulent would be an understatement.

The glory days of Jack Walker's ownership had long gone when the Indian poultry giants purchased the Lancashire outfit from Walker's trust in 2010, but under their guidance, relegation from the Premier League came in 2012 - and they've never returned.

Despite the Rao family not attending a match since January 2013, they still continue to fund the club's annual losses, even if in recent times there has been struggles getting money out of their native India and to the UK, but they have a lot of finance, and as per a report from The News in 2025, the Venky's were worth an estimated £1.56 billion.

With restrictions from the High Court of Delhi now lifted ahead of 2026-27, could the Venky's be ready to invest more into the club's squad to guide them away from another potential relegation battle? Only time will tell...

The Coates family have been involved in Stoke City since 1986, when Peter Coates became majority shareholder and chairman, but after selling to an Icelandic consortium in 1999, he would return seven years later, and in 2015, his son John would become vice-chairman.

Coates junior has worked his way up the ranks since, and in 2024, a change to the ownership structure saw John become sole owner of the club, and he is the one pulling the strings and making the big decisions on who comes and goes at the bet365 Stadium.

Bet365 has been a big business win for the Coates family, and as per Forbes' real-time net worth analysis, Coates himself is worth an estimated £2.3 billion, and despite Stoke's stagnation in the Championship recently, there's seemingly no sign of the funding stopping at the Staffordshire club.

QPR have had turbulent ownership changes over the years, with the eras of Flavio Briatore and Bernie Ecclestone, then Tony Fernandes well and truly in the past.

The current ownership group at Loftus Road is mainly led by Malaysian businessman Ruben Gnanalingam, who owns just under 60 per cent of the Hoops, US investor Richard Reilly holds 21 per cent and former chairman Amit Bhatia also holding 20 per cent.

Whilst Gnanalingam's personal wealth isn't known, the family wealth sits at a healthy £2.3 billion, as per Forbes, with much of their successful business coming from the shipping industry in the form of Westports Holdings.

Gnanalingam is the executive chairman and group managing director of the company, with the 49-year-old being a part of the furniture in the QPR hierarchy since the Fernandes days in 2011 - that doesn't look set to change anytime soon, and with signings recently such as Richard Kone and Ronnie Edwards, he's definitely been putting his money where his mouth is.

From the outside looking in, it may represent something of a surprise that, in Steve Lansdown, Bristol City have the fifth-richest owner at Championship level.

The Bristolian has held his position as chairman of the Robins since 2002, and is the founder of Bristol Sport alongside coupling his duties at Ashton Gate with a majority shareholder role with both Bristol Bears and Bristol Flyers.

This means that, as per Forbes, Lansdown's net worth sits at a whopping £2.38 billion. However, in contrast to other counterparts both on this list and outside of it, the 73-year-old's personal wealth has hardly been translated into a healthy transfer warchest for the Robins to utilise over the years.

Lansdown has undertaken serious work away from the field to help Bristol City grow, such as the impressive £40 million redevelopment of Ashton Gate around a decade ago, although his lack of investment into the playing squad has been a frequent point of frustration for supporters.

The Robins have significant potential and all the foundations are in place for that to be achieved, bar one - you do get the impression that they need to finally make a real splash on signings to get to that next level and threaten to head to the Premier League for the first time in the club's long history.

When you look at how Portsmouth owner Michael Eisner has made his billions, it's no great shock that few second-tier sides can boast a wealthier chief than the New York native - even if, once again, that isn't always reflected in the club's transfer activity.

As per the Los Angeles Business Journal in 2024, Eisner has accumulated a net worth of $3.8 billion (£2.83 billion), having formerly held the role of Disney's CEO for more than 20 years before entering the world of football ownership by taking over Pompey nine years ago.

FLW takes a look at which Championship clubs have the highest wage bill heading into the new season

John Mousinho hasn't been totally without backing in recent years, and Pompey's exile from the Championship has ended under Eisner's ownership, but his investment continues to be patient and methodical without throwing everything towards bringing Premier League football back to Fratton Park - although the summer of 2026 has definitely seen a more 'shackles off' approach at Fratton Park.

We know far less about Charlton Athletic owner Joshua Friedman, who is the majority shareholder of a consortium - Global Football Partners - that completed a takeover to purchase the South London outfit from Thomas Sandgaard in the summer of 2023.

Indeed, official net worth estimates of Friedman are sparse. However, if claims of $5 billion (£3.72 billion) are to be believed, then Charlton very much have a wealthy board, and that has shone through in recent times.

The Addicks' investment has amplified since returning to the Championship through the League One play-offs in May of last year, which preceded - as per Transfermarkt - a summer spending spree under Nathan Jones worth €11.62 million (£10.07 million) as the likes of Rob Apter, Thomas Kaminski, Charlie Kelman and Harvey Knibbs all made their way to the Valley and ultimately helped to keep the side afloat.

There are a number of shareholders on the board at West Ham, and until June, the two largest stakes belonged to controversial chairman David Sullivan and his family, and Daniel Kretinsky, at 38.8% and 27% respectively.

That changed though recently, with Czech businessman Kretinsky and his consortium's stake rising to 43 per cent after a deal was struck with Vanessa Gold - and that could lead to a more ambitious Hammers going into 2026-27.

As per the Sunday Times Rich List of 2025 (via the BBC), Kretinsky's wealth comes in at $10 billion (£8.68 billion), which is a clear second in the Championship when it comes to riches.

Sullivan has ploughed heavy investment into West Ham over the years, much of which was done alongside the late David Gold, although he's hardly a popular figure at the London Stadium owing to the decision to move out of Upton Park and, more recently, a seemingly-endless series of big-money signings who have failed to deliver - and that's ultimately why the East Londoners will be playing Championship football next season for the first time since 2012.

Under Kretinsky's largest stakeholding though, things may be different, and fans will hope that the owner of Czech energy company EPH and also the owner of Royal Mail will actually be a majority owner when Sullivan decides he wants to sell up his share entirely.

Wolves have the richest owners in the Championship overall, with the Old Gold enduring something of a rollercoaster journey throughout the Fosun Group's 10-year reign in charge at Molineux.

Made up of majority stakeholder Guo Guanchang, Fosun Group CEO Wang Qunbin and interim executive chairman Nathan Shi, Fosun's net worth is not publicly known. The Chinese conglomerate is seriously wealthy, though, and according to Companies Market Cap in June 2025, the total net worth sits at a stunning £20.53 billion.

Who is the best Championship owner? Let us know in the comments!

Following a tumultous couple of years in the Premier League, which saw Wolves relegated in dead-last in 25/26, the Molineux faithful will now be hoping that Fosun can show the same level of investment and ambition back in the Championship just as they did when the side last graced the division, going up as champions after signing the likes of Ruben Neves and the late Diogo Jota.

#2146. Charlton Athletic boss Nathan Jones left unhappy at FA rule change

Charlton Athletic boss Nathan Jones is one of "a number" of head coaches in the top four leagues in England to have objected to the yellow card rules bestowed upon managers.

It has been the case since the 2018/19 campaign that cautions could be shown to the dugouts in the EFL, with the Premier League following suit the following year. Traditionally, a manager would have to receive four yellow cards before they would receive a touchline ban.

However, in an update to the rules ahead of the 2023/24 term, the FA introduced a 'Participant Behaviour Charter' to maintain a positive environment across the top-flight, the EFL, the Women's Super League and the National League System (Steps 1-4).

The new charter brought in the current rule that a manager only has to be shown three yellow cards to receive a touchline ban, with the cut-off point being the 37th game of the season. Unsurprisingly, Jones, one of the more animated coaches in the game, is against that.

According to Standard Sport, Charlton boss Nathan Jones is among several head coaches who don't like the reduction to how many yellow cards lead to a touchline for a manager.

Managers across the 92 had a meeting recently to discuss the effectiveness of these rules, and Jones, among others, believes that the threshold is incredibly short, especially with managers under much more pressure and scrutiny than ever before, it seems.

Additionally, in comparison to players, who pick up a one-match suspension for five yellow cards by the 19th game of the season, and a two-match ban if they collect 10 by the 37th, a touchline ban for just three cautions by the 37th for a manager seems incredibly harsh.

Head coaches find themselves booked if they are caught out of their technical areas, and they also receive yellow cards if there are any infringements in the dugout that a referee cannot attribute to one person. As the 'most senior' member of staff, they are the ones responsible for controlling things.

Nathan Jones will be one of the few managers who fall victim to these cards, and he is understandably looking for the rules to be relaxed.

Ultimately, certain head coaches will feel that these rules almost limit how well they can do their jobs, especially someone like Jones, who isn't afraid to let his emotions show.

The Welshman was seen almost joining a late attack against Hull City last season, and hung off the dugout after his side scored a late equaliser against Burnley this term, among other crazy things that only someone like Jones is associated with.

Having his actions limited on the sidelines may be detrimental to how much he can motivate his side, especially given how infrequent these sanctions can be.

FLW has debated which 15 fanbases generate the best atmospheres and more inside their home stadiums

Not every departure from a technical area is a yellow card, as Arsenal boss Mikel Arteta, another very animated manager, rightly pointed out in late October after he admitted he was clipping up infringements from his fellow coaches after he was accused of spending more time out of his little box than others were.

So, managers already feel as if they are walking on eggshells; such is the nature of the job, but sanctions like these can often make it even more of a nervous place.

However, despite Jones and other managers' grumbles, the FA can point to a 17% decrease in technical area cautions since the start of the 2023/24 campaign when the three-yellow-card rule was implemented.

What do you think about the manager yellow card rules? Let us know in the comments.

The numbers indicate that things are working, but again, managers may point to the infrequency of the rules as the reason for that. Not every infringement should be a yellow card, but the reasons why they are being handed out aren't something that coaches agree on.

Time will tell whether the Charlton boss, and others who oppose the rule change, will have their way and see the yellow card threshold increase once more, but with the success of the charter so far, it may be difficult for them to persuade the FA.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

#2145. Leeds United, Nottingham Forest set sights on potential deal for Watford prospect

Watford are on the lookout for new players to bring into their ranks, but they’ve found some fresh prospects in the academy setup too. Amin Nabizada has made a name for himself this term, and among those hoping to follow in his footsteps is Jamael John-Baptiste.

However, the Hornets could have a fight on their hands in the bid to keep the teenage striker. Football Insider reports that Premier League pair Leeds United and Nottingham Forest are both interested in poaching John-Baptiste from the setup at Vicarage Road.

Watford hope to retain John-Baptiste, but his Premier League admirers are watching developments closely ahead of a potential swoop. He signed scholarship terms ahead of the 2025/26 season, and with no professional deal in place, it is believed that his contract runs out at the end of the current campaign.

Watford have given John-Baptiste a chance to prove himself after spells in the Spurs and Brentford academies, and he has delivered for the U18s. That could help them in their bid to keep him, as he may well be eager to pay back those who gave him a chance – much like Sydie Peck at Sheffield United.

However, Leeds United and Nottingham Forest are attractive clubs with strong academy setups. Not only have plenty of prospects come through their ranks and into the first-team squad over the years, but they will be able to offer elite facilities and a higher level of coaching than the Hornets.

If their interest does firm up, it could make keeping John-Baptiste a tough task. For now though, he will remain focused on delivering in the Watford academy to strengthen his reputation and continue his progression.

John-Baptiste is clearly a bright prospect, and one who Watford may well hope breaks into the first-team.

However, it has been a rarity for academy-reared prospects to break through at Vicarage Road. Nabizada has done so this term, but otherwise, the likes of Albert Eames and James Clarridge have only really been called upon to offer depth.

If the Hornets want to have a better chance of retaining talents like John-Baptiste for the foreseeable, they have to forge a clearer route to the first team. If not, then clubs of Leeds United and Nottingham Forest’s ilk will be able to poach youngsters.

Time will tell what comes of John-Baptiste’s situation though, as interest starts to emerge.

#2144. West Ham, Millwall & Norwich City pushing to sign English defender

West Ham United, Millwall, and Norwich City will each have their sights firmly fixed on a place in the Championship top eight this season, and as the January window approaches, the trio are set to battle it out with Premier League clubs for the signature of a highly-rated defender.

Whilst neither West Ham, Millwall, nor Norwich occupy one of the top eight places in the Championship right now, this will not deter either of them from forcing their way into those places come May 2027.

It hasn't been a totally ideal start to the season for any of those clubs, and as the January window grows closer, attention will be swiftly turning to transfer business, where these three sides are set to go head-to-head.

Football League World has asked AI to predict the 2027/28 EFL Championship table if Manchester United and Manchester City were in it

According to TEAMtalk, West Ham, Millwall, and Norwich are keeping close tabs on Stevenage centre-back Jack Taylor, with the Englishman currently thriving out on loan with Exeter City in League Two.

However, the Championship trio face competition from Premier League sides such as Brighton and Hove Albion, Everton, Brentford, Crystal Palace, and Fulham, with Taylor's performances in the fourth tier not going unnoticed.

Taylor has never played above the fourth tier in his career, aged 21, and the jump to the Premier League or Championship at this stage would be an incredibly jarring one, but one he could be ready to make.

Taylor was on the youth books of Chelsea as a youngster, but decided to quit academy football and pursue his own route into the professional game, where he was eventually noticed by Sutton United whilst playing for his college.

A loan to the sixth tier in 2024/25 with Hampton and Richmond gave Taylor his first regular taste of first-team football, before breaking into the Sutton side after six months on loan.

His performances in the National League caught the attention of Stevenage, who signed Taylor in January 2026 before sending him out on loan to League Two Exeter City in the summer, where he has thrived in the fourth tier.

Yet to make a senior appearance for the Boro, Taylor is starring for the Grecians in Devon, as Stevenage may well have hit the jackpot on a player who may well never play a game for them if a January deal is agreed.

Would you like to see your club sign Jack Taylor? How high do you think his ceiling is? Share your thoughts below!

With West Ham facing competition in their own division for the signature of Taylor, Parker's side will find it increasingly difficult to secure his services if Premier League clubs are lingering.

The Hammers lack depth and youth in the middle of their back line, with both Konstantinos Mavropanos and Max Kilman closing in on 30, and no real prospects coming through their academy.

Parker will be looking to make some additions in January, though whether Taylor will be one of them remains to be seen.

#2143. ‘I am sure…’ – John Aldridge sets Alexander Isak goal tally target for rest of the season

John Aldridge has specified what he feels is a realistic goal tally target for Alexander Isak to try and hit for Liverpool this season.

After an injury-ravaged debut campaign at Anfield in which he netted just four times in 22 appearances, the Swedish marksman has doubled that return in his first 14 outings in 2026/27, scoring seven times in nine Premier League games (Transfermarkt).

Having cost a club-record fee of £125m to sign from Newcastle in the summer of 2025, the 27-year-old has been repaying that outlay with his consistent goalscoring form since August, accounting for 50% of the Reds’ top-flight goal tally for the season so far.

In his latest column for the Liverpool Echo, Aldridge voiced his confidence in Isak to maintain the excellent form that he’s been showing since the start of the campaign.

The former Reds striker wrote: ‘If Liverpool are to have a successful season, then a lot will rest on the goals of Alexander Isak.

‘Now up to eight for the season, the striker just needs to keep getting in the positions and if the chances come, like they have been doing, then more often than not you expect him to finish them off.’

In terms of a goal target for the campaign, Aldridge specified: ‘Having scored eight by this point of the season, I am sure he will be targeting the 20-25-goal tally now.’

(Photo by Justin Setterfield/Getty Images)

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

Provided that Isak avoids a recurrence of the injury issues which plagued him last term and extrapolates his current form over a full campaign, there’s no reason why he can’t match Aldridge’s target by the end of May.

With eight goals in 14 matches up to this point, maintaining that return would see Liverpool’s number 9 reach the 20-goal mark in 35 games, and get to 25 goals inside 44 appearances.

As it stands, the Reds will play a bare minimum of 50 matches across the campaign – realistically that number will be closer to 55-60 with progression in the Champions League and domestic cups.

With that in mind, it’s not inconceivable that Isak could get close to 30 goals if he stays fit and in form, although he could be rotated more frequently once Hugo Ekitike returns from injury over the winter, as is currently projected.

Liverpool’s greatest ever goalscorer in Ian Rush rightly said that finding the net is ‘all that matters’ for a centre-forward, and the Sweden international has been doing that in abundance this season.

Barring a cruel twist of fate, we’re sure that the 27-year-old will top the 2o-goal mark by the end of the campaign. At this rate, he could hit it by the end of February.

#2142. Chelsea Monitor Jarrod Bowen And Crysencio Summerville

Chelsea’s wide-player search has moved back into focus after the Guardian reported that the club are monitoring West Ham pair Jarrod Bowen and Crysencio Summerville.

The update lands during a busy early market for Xabi Alonso’s incoming rebuild.

The Guardian’s latest transfer round-up states that Chelsea like both West Ham forwards, while an earlier report noted Chelsea and Manchester United had tracked Summerville.

That same earlier update also said Aston Villa, Chelsea and Everton had expressed interest in Bowen.

West Ham pulling away at Stamford Bridge 👀Jarrod Bowen stuns Chelsea early before Crysencio Summerville doubles the lead. pic.twitter.com/Ktwr4pKZGH

Alonso officially begins work on July 1, with Chelsea confirming he has signed a four-year contract to lead the men’s team.

The winger search is not just about adding more attacking names. It is about identifying profiles who can quickly give Chelsea sharper final-third decisions.

Bowen would offer immediate Premier League output, penalty-box timing and a direct threat from the right.

Summerville, meanwhile, fits the younger, explosive one-v-one profile that would sit neatly inside Alonso’s longer-term wing plan.

Read Chelsea has already tracked the separate Summerville angle, but this latest dual link sharpens the picture.

Chelsea appear to be testing two different solutions to the same problem: proven reliability or higher-upside acceleration.

With pre-season closing in, that is exactly the sort of market check Alonso needs before deciding how much of Chelsea’s attacking rebuild should lean on speed, touchline width and more ruthless wide output.
